ParlaMint-EE_2016-12-14_U305	Today's 10th item is the continuation of the second reading of the Income Tax Act, the Social Tax Act and the Act amending other laws 302. I would like to ask Mr Stalnukhin, Chairman of the Monetary Commission, for a presentation by the commission.
ParlaMint-EE_2016-12-14_U306	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Ladies and gentlemen, The second reading of the draft was on the agenda on 7 December, a week ago. The second reading was suspended on a proposal from the Commission and the deadline for amendments was set at 16 a.m. on 9 December. Now I am talking about what the Commission has done during this week. Aivar Sõerd, member of the Riigikogu, the Group of the Conservative People's Party of Estonia, the Group of the Estonian Reform Party and the Group of the Estonian Free Party submitted proposals within the deadline for amendments. At the meeting of the Monetary Commission on 12 December, the Estonian Free Party withdrew part of the amendments. Here I must make it clear that, first of all, there is a draft 327 of the Estonian Free Party faction in the proceedings before the Riigikogu, according to which the planned increase in VAT on accommodation services in 2017 would be cancelled. There is also an amendment to Bill 302 with the same objective. The Committee on Monetary Affairs would have liked to have combined the draft Free Party with the draft we are discussing today, of course, if the Free Party had agreed to it. However, this could not be done because Bill 327 has not yet passed the first reading. However, you can only connect on this condition. In addition, the Estonian Free Party Group withdrew five amendments to the tax changes related to the registration of cars at its meeting on 12 December. The representative of the political group explained that, as these proposals have been fully taken into account, the Commission has excluded all these amendments to the State Fees Act from the draft and that there is no need to draw attention to this issue in plenary, it will withdraw these proposals. The same group also withdrew two amendments whose need for amendment by the Commission fell due to 142. In particular, the Commission decided that the tax credit for health promotion would be enforced indefinitely and that the requirement to provide a fixed-term analysis would be waived. Preparatory meetings for the second reading of the bill took place in the Committee on Monetary Affairs on 8, 12 and 13 December. In addition to the ordinary meeting of the Committee on Monetary Affairs, an extraordinary meeting was held on 12 December, with representatives of stakeholders invited to take part. The circle of topics was next. The first issue concerned the accelerated procedure for amendments to the Tax Code. The main participants in this debate were the Estonian Employers' Confederation and the Estonian Chamber of Commerce and Industry. The Association of Estonian Beer Producers took the floor on raising the excise duty rate for beer and, after that, the Estonian Chamber of Commerce and Industry. The Estonian Gas Association, the Estonian Printing and Packaging Industry Association, Estonian Cell and the Estonian Food Industry Association spoke about the increase in the excise rate of natural gas. Last but not least, the fourth issue was increasing the rate of excise duty on heavy fuel oil. This topic was taken up by United Oil OÜ. The Estonian Association of Small and Medium Enterprises took part in this meeting and expressed its opinion. Everything that happened at this meeting is quite thoroughly reflected in the explanatory memorandum that you can get from the shelf at the back of the hall any time. I will not question your reading skills, as was the case here when we discussed the previous issue. So if you want, you can get to know it. But let us now go to the amendments. Amendments 1, 3, 4, 7 and 133 have been tabled by the Group of the Estonian Free Party. The aim is to gradually increase the income tax rate, which would cover an increase in the tax-free minimum so that slightly more than 80% of workers would receive higher incomes. Amendments 2, 129, 151 and 157 are tabled by the Reform Party Group. In presenting these proposals, the representative of the group explained that the provisions to be deleted pursuant to these amendments could not be supported, inter alia, because they had not been drawn up in accordance with the good practice of proceeding with the draft. In particular, stakeholders have not been involved and impact assessments have not been carried out. I am taking this opportunity to say that the impact analysis we received from the Ministry of Finance is in the explanatory memorandum to the bill, starting on page 8. Amendments 5 and 6 have been tabled by Mr Aivar Sõerd, member of the Riigikogu. In Amendment 5, the author explains that since dividends in Estonia are not subject to income tax at the level of an individual, it is not justified to double taxation of such dividends by means of a tax-free minimum. Amendment 6 provides that the refinancing of tax-free income could take place with a step of EUR 2 instead of the EUR 1.8 proposed in the draft. The representative of the initiator explained that EUR 1,8 was due to the need to bring tax-free income to zero at a level of EUR 2100 per month. Amendment 8 has been tabled by the Committee on Monetary Affairs. It aims to amend Paragraph 231 (1) of the Income Tax Act and delete the reference to the Family Act in order to allow the use of additional tax-free income for any person who is factually dependent on the child. Amendments 9 to 128 have been tabled by the Group of the Estonian Reform Party. The content of these proposals is to increase the amount of an additional tax-free pension of EUR 2700 provided for in § 232 of the Income Tax Act by EUR 1 in each proposal. Amendments 130 and 131 are tabled by the Estonian Free Party Group. The objective of the proposal 130 is to maintain the right to deduct, in full or in part, the interest of the parent who has taken parental leave during the tax period, who is raising the child alone. Proposal 131 recommends that no change should be made to the reduction of the marginal interest rate on housing loans from EUR 1200 to EUR 300. Amendments 132 and 134 are proposed by the Committee on Monetary Affairs. Proposal 132 allows spouses who use the collective property regime to jointly account for certain costs and deductions when declaring income. According to the supplement, spouses can count jointly on the additional tax-free income for child maintenance, the training costs paid during the tax period and the interest on the mortgage. Tax-free income cannot be transferred to another spouse, and this also applies to charitable donations and voluntary pension insurance contributions. Amendment 134 allows for more flexible account to be taken of tax-free payments of uneven amounts. Amendment 135 is tabled by the EKRE Group and consists of the submission of a joint income tax return by the spouses. The 136th amendment, tabled by Aivar Sõerd, a member of the Riigikogu, is identical to Amendment 137 tabled by the Group of the Estonian Free Party. Both aim to remove from the draft the amendment to Paragraph 44 (2) of the Income Tax Act, which repeals the possibility of submitting a joint income tax return for spouses. Amendments 138 and 139 of the Estonian Free Party are motivated by the same objective. Amendments 140, 141, 142, 143 and 145 concern the regulation relating to expenditure on health promotion. According to Amendment 141 of the Committee on Finance, the employer will continue to be entitled to benefit from tax-free private health insurance. This proposal has also been supported by the Estonian Insurance Association, the Estonian Employers' Confederation and the Estonian Trade Union Confederation in their opinions to the Committee on Monetary Affairs. The tax relief foreseen for health promotion will be put into effect for an unlimited period and the obligation to provide a fixed-term analysis will be waived. Specifically on this subject is Amendment 142. Similar changes were proposed in the proposals 140, 143 and 145 submitted by the Estonian Free Party Group, which were supported, in part or in substance, by the Commission. Amendments 146, 147 and 148 tabled by the Group of the Estonian Free Party provide for the introduction of a social tax ceiling. The representative of the initiator of the draft act explained that Latvia's experience shows that the introduction of a social tax ceiling does not contribute to the creation of high-paid jobs. At the same time, it is a very expensive measure. Amendment 149 is tabled by the Group of the Estonian Reform Party, which aims not to give up the reduction of the social tax rate and to provide for a reduction of the social tax rate to 30% by 2019 in the Social Tax Act. This rate would then fall by half a percentage point in 2017, by one and a half in 2018 and by another one percentage point in 2019. Amendment 152 is tabled by the Group of the Estonian Conservative People's Party and aims to remove all provisions concerning the rate of excise duty on natural gas from the draft. By amendment 153, the Committee on Monetary Affairs decided to delete § 8 from the draft, which provided for the amendment of the state fee for the registration of the vehicle or for the change of the owner of the vehicle. Amendment 154 relates to draft 301 pending before the Riigikogu, which passed the third reading on 14 December and cannot be changed again. Its formulation has taken into account the reduction of the social tax rate by one per cent since 2018. As Bill 302 seeks to cancel the reduction of social security contributions, this implies the need to amend Bill 301 amending the Health Insurance Act and the Social Tax Act. By Amendment 156, the Group of the Estonian Free Party wishes to postpone the withdrawal of social tax by half a year. The Finance Committee asked the Ministry of Finance to provide an impact analysis based on changes to the text of the draft at second reading. The impact analyses of the fiscal policy amendments submitted to the Committee on Monetary Affairs are annexed to this explanatory memorandum. At its meeting on 13 December 2016, the Committee on Monetary Affairs decided to propose that the second reading of the draft be terminated. Thank you.

ParlaMint-EE_2016-12-14_U338	Thank you. I am forced to return to the joint declarations because your answer was not precise and exhaustive. ParlaMint-EE_2016-12-14_U371	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Honourable members of the Riigikogu! I asked the management board of the Riigikogu to present the draft before you as a co-rapporteur in order to discuss the amendments to the draft 302 under discussion and to answer the questions concerning them. As you know, this draft initially covered only the amendments to the Income Tax Act and the Social Tax Act relating to the promotion of workers' health, which I presented at first reading on 19 October. In the course of the procedure, they have been accompanied by urgent changes resulting from the agreement of the Government Union, and these are the following: The government union agreement, which is the basis for the changes, was signed on 21 November, a little more than a month before the end of the year. Indeed, this will result in a rapid pace of dealing with some of the changes. You will probably agree with me that political parties represented in the parliament in Estonia as a democratic country have the right to agree on the formation of a new government and that each government must have the opportunity, within the limits of law, to implement its policies. The new government union wants only tax changes that are fast due to the date of entry into force and which do not require major legislative changes to be approved in December. With a budget volume of 9.5 billion next year, the rapidly enforceable changes would be around 1%, or EUR 0.1 billion. The changes that are rapidly enforceable are also limited to those whose implementation does not require long preparation, as they are not technically complex. I would point out, for example, that if any tax rate remains exactly the same as a result of a legislative amendment, its implementation cannot be too burdensome for anyone. Good Riigikogu! I will now give an overview of the proposed changes. Most importantly, we want to carry out a tax-free income reform, i.e. to increase the tax-free income of people earning a exponentially lower average salary. This reform reduces the burden of labour tax for people who receive lower and medium wages, who are too highly taxed in Estonia compared to other countries, and who therefore find it difficult to cope and remain competitive. Raising tax-free income to EUR 500 to people earning average wages favours that those who work can cope with their pay and do not have to depend on public subsidies. In this context, in the Labour Market Services and Benefits Act, we wish to repeal the substantive provisions on income tax refunds for low-paid people so that in 2017 these people will receive a tax refund for 2016 and in 2018 a new tax reform will be fully implemented. Increasing tax-free income will reduce the tax burden on lower and middle incomes by more than half a percentage point in the reduction of social tax on labour taxes. The Government Union therefore wishes to keep the social tax rate the same as it is at present. Thus, the reduction in social tax by 0.5% would be avoided, which, without amending the law, would apply from next year. The legislator has the possibility to replace one tax measure with another, in particular if it is a more effective measure than before. As regards the increase in tax-free income, there are still some changes to the Income Tax Act. The limit on the deduction of interest on a mortgage from the taxable income is set at EUR 300 and the exemption for interest on deposits is abolished. With regard to the joint declarations, we have come to a solution by considering the possibilities that we will retain the possibility of a joint deduction of costs. Unlike today, when submitting a joint declaration, the tax-free income of spouses cannot be aggregated, but the new tax-free rate of EUR 500 per month exceeds the maximum level of tax-free income that could have been used under the current procedure for the submission of a joint declaration by spouses. The second, as well as the urgent change due to the deadline, is to keep the VAT rate on accommodation services at the current level, at 9%, leaving aside the planned increase to 14%. I read yesterday Verni Loodmaa, Chairman of the Board of the Estonian Hotels and Restaurant Association, that the feedback from the members of the association to the decision of the new coalition is supportive. Mr Loedmaa says: 'My deep conviction is that the budget will receive much more tax revenue from our sector in the coming years than it would have had in the event of an increase in VAT. The proposed changes favour new investments and leave room for possible wage increases, which in some cases are essential. The decisions taken will help Loodmaa to maintain existing jobs and create new jobs. Thirdly, it is planned to increase the rates of excise duty on beer, cider and wine for dilute alcoholic beverages by reducing the difference with excise duties on spirits. The current excise policy and the relatively rapid increase in wages in recent years have led to the same level of relative vodka prices, while beer has become relatively cheap compared to the average income. The increase in the planned beer excise duty will prevent further relative depreciation. In the long term, it affects people's health, their ability to work and should increase the number of years of healthy life. It is also in line with the Green Paper on alcohol policy, which provides for the taxation of alcohol irrespective of the way it is produced. In addition, it is planned to postpone the increase in alcohol duty of 1 January 2017 and 2018 until February. Fourthly, changes to excise duties on fuels are planned. It is planned to cancel the increase in excise duty on diesel for 2018. In addition, excise duties on natural gas are planned to be increased between 2018 and 2020. As a result, the final price of natural gas will increase by 2,5 to 5,4% in each given year as compared to the previous year, depending on the volume of the consumer's gas consumption. Given the feedback that the rise in excise rates is problematic, in particular by weakening the competitive position of industry, the government has promised to look for ways to mitigate excise policy on energy-intensive companies in the first half of the new year. From 1 July 2017, a package on the harmonisation of excise duties on gaseous fuels will be implemented. It is a long-prepared change, also discussed with stakeholders, which is mainly necessary for taxation on a similar basis to gaseous motor fuels and improves the competitive environment. These are the main changes related to taxes included in Bill 302. As I said at the beginning, in December only the part of the proposals of the government union that is really urgent will be dealt with. The rapid pace does not mean that changes are made without taking into account proposals and criticisms. The feedback received has been taken into account in a number of important points. For example, the possibility of joint income declarations is maintained, the new regulation of the vehicle registration fee was removed from the draft. Basically and technically more complex drafts are planned by the government to prepare and deal with the normal procedure. Their impact starts in 2018 and later. The reduction of the income tax rate applied to companies in the distribution of profits, the creation of an entrepreneurial account, the taxation of sweetened beverages, the introduction of bank charges and a number of other steps will be prepared and dealt with in the next year. This list also includes the linking of the state fee for vehicle registration with the CO2 emission or engine power of the vehicle. The Ministry of Finance has drawn up a proposal for this, but some of the nuances that have emerged during this work require longer consideration and it is therefore more appropriate not to regulate this issue with today's draft. Happy Members of the Riigikogu! Growth is one of the main objectives of the government union. As the economy grows, people's well-being grows faster, we can help weaker members of society, we can improve our defence capacity more quickly and we can help more families. We want to focus attention on reducing labour taxes; we want to focus more attention on the lower and middle earners. Given that the tax rate will remain low, 20% even for higher wages, and most workers will pay less labour tax in the future thanks to increased tax-free income, we see the incentive effect of reforms primarily on employment and the economy at all. Thank you for your attention. I'm ready to answer your questions.

ParlaMint-EE_2016-12-14_U467	Thank you, very good question! Impact analyses were not only carried out on border trade. The income of excise duties on alcohol depends on how much people drink. The second factor is what you were talking about, perhaps border trade, and the third is the secret market. The secret market is always taken into account by decision-makers in order not to create a situation where the share of the secret market is increasing through certain activities. If we look at the collection of alcohol duty this year, we can see that it has been received in the expected volume, even a little more. We were more conservative when we calculated it last year. If we look at next year, given that the price of beer is rising significantly, it is estimated that at least in the second half of the year the quantities will decrease by 10%, of which 6% is more or less likely to have an impact on border trade and 4% will have a better health behaviour.

ParlaMint-EE_2016-12-14_U504	Good chairman of the sitting! Actually, you can talk about this bill for quite a while, but I'll ask you five plus three minutes, because no more format will allow.
ParlaMint-EE_2016-12-14_U505	There are a number of issues in this tax package, but I will focus on one of them. This concerns gas excise duty, which is now rising and rising by 25% each year for three years in a row. This is subsections 4 and 38 to 40 of the draft law. Why do I think it's necessary to talk about it? Remember when the coalition agreement was concluded, the government said that it would put an end to the economic stagnation we are in and will certainly focus on steps that will contribute to economic growth. Industry certainly plays a major role in our economy. I therefore found it a little unclear how we can increase our economy by increasing excise duties on gas for three years in a row, i.e. by increasing the cost of companies. I don't think so. The proposed increase in the rate of excise duty on natural gas is to a large extent detrimental to the international competitiveness of production companies that consume larger quantities of natural gas. As I said, this change will increase the cost base for companies and, as a result, the investment of companies and the number of employees may be reduced, which will ultimately result in less money being received into the state budget. I would also like to point out that many companies have invested heavily in using natural gas as the most environmentally friendly fossil fuel in their production processes. When they made investments, they could not have foreseen that the excise duty on natural gas would be increased by 25% per year over the next three years. Such a change will also have a negative impact on the environment, as the increase in the price of natural gas resulting from an increase in the rate of excise duty could increase the use of other fuels which are more environmentally harmful in industrial plants. Why am I talking about this? Perhaps it seems as if the Minister's answers turned out that I am from the opposition party and I want to delay the hearing. But it's not really like that. This is not just a spiteful opinion from the representative of the Reform Party Group, who wants to delay the sitting in any case. Our goal is to draw attention to stupidity. We are not alone because the Committee on Monetary Affairs invited the draft to a debate, where it was decided, among other things, that there would be an increase in excise duty on natural gas, also representatives of the Estonian Association of Gas, the Estonian Association of Printing and Packaging Industry, the largest energy consumer AS Estonian Cell and the Estonian Food Industry Association. I am quoting their views, just in case, you will understand that this is not just my opinion or the opinion of my party, but is actually the general opinion of the experts concerned. For example, the Estonian Gaasing Association takes the view: 'The impact of the tax increase is very wide-ranging and disproportionate and discriminatory in one type of heating. " He also writes that the changes will bring about a situation where fuels that are much more polluting than natural gas and are less energy valuable are taxed times lower. The Estonian Printing and Packaging Industry Association found that, as a result of increasing energy costs, companies are losing investment in both production development and capacity building, and due to the tax burden, the competitiveness of the processing industry, including the energy-intensive industry, is low compared to neighbouring countries. The owners of Estonian Cell made one of the biggest investments in Estonia in 2006, about 153 million euros, and their turnover per year is around 70 to 80 million euros. We would like to see more of these companies in Estonia, so that investments come and our industry develops. Estonian Cell believes that its additional contribution alone next year and every subsequent year, as well as the increase in excise duty, is EUR 500000. Consequently, this company's cost base is increasing. Do you think such steps will encourage the emergence of industrial enterprises or new investments in the same order of magnitude as Estonian Cell? I also mention the opinion of the Estonian Food Industry Association, who said that the impact of all tax increases on the final consumer is such that, in essence, this draft threatens an increase in food prices. You gotta think about that, too. ParlaMint-EE_2016-12-14_U508	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Ladies and gentlemen, If the previous speaker, Deniss, spoke first of all about the substance of the draft, I would like to speak more about the rules surrounding our legislation. As a member of the long-term Committee on Legal Affairs and now, for a little more than a year and a half as chairman of the Committee on Constitutional Affairs, I am very sad to note that we are moving more and more of the borders in which we should set an example for the people and, instead of complying with the laws, we find reasons for not following them. Today's major parliamentary parties have unanimously adopted 'Towards the development of justice policy until 2018', according to which the legislative process must be predictable and open. However, in recent times, the Riigikogu's plenary hall has been a situation where the two principles mentioned are not met at all. If, under the Tax Administration Act, tax changes have to be adopted six months earlier, the law is currently unambiguously broken. After all, the goal is to allow sufficient time to adapt to those who are subject to the change. This has been emphasised by the business representative organisations of the Chamber of Commerce and Employers' Confederation as well as by the Chancellor of Justice and, of course, by the previous President. At times, it seems that Finance Minister Sven Sester is making tax changes as if the elephant was behind the pieces in the porcelain shop. The urgency of the draft will lead to a situation where the quality of legislation suffers and the effects of the changes have not been sufficiently assessed. A vivid example of this is the abandonment of car tax. There is also much uncertainty about the abolition of the joint declaration. The Management Board of the Riigikogu has approved the guidelines for good practice in lawmaking; on this basis, there must be a reasonable time for the draft law to be processed, including for discussion in the lead committee, for preparation of documents and for discussion in plenary. During this period, members of the Riigikogu will be able to formulate their views on the draft and amendments and stakeholders will be able to express their views. According to the same instructions, the commission decides to place the draft on the agenda of the Riigikogu for the second reading of the working week of the Riigikogu after examining all the amendments and taking decisions on them. In recent weeks, it has happened repeatedly that the draft has long been sent to the Chamber for second reading, but the amendments have not yet been discussed. Very bad practice, another very bad practice. After all, the processing of the bill is a reasonable time taken from the members of the Riigikogu to examine tax changes and to shape the position. According to the Riigikogu's Home and Rules of Procedure Act, a member of the Riigikogu has the opportunity to present his or her amendments at the committee meeting. This bill was not allowed again. I also asked the chairman of the committee about this. He said that, yes, after the interruption, the author of the amendment no longer wanted to present his proposal. I'd rather think Jüri Jaanson just didn't realize he had that chance. Similarly, the proposed changes have not passed the normal round of coordination within the Government of the Republic and have not been introduced to the relevant business organisations. More time, please!

ParlaMint-EE_2016-12-14_U532	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Ladies and gentlemen, I think that a little something positive should also be brought between the great criticisms. After all, we remember how we only recently adopted a cluster bill to change the budget here, and we also saw how we did the night sittings, protesting against the measures that were then used under the leadership of the Reform Party. I think, in this situation, we've been acting, I'd say, just coming to meet people. The expectations of the Estonian people for the new government are high and very many of the changes to the budget will benefit. The biggest change concerns about 85% of employees who will have much more money available on payday. From 2018, the income tax-free minimum will rise from the current EUR 170 to EUR 500, while the income tax rate will not change. Analysts have calculated that, thanks to this step, people earning up to 1200 euros a month will have 62 euros more. This makes an additional annual income of EUR 744, which people are most likely to use to buy food and consumer goods, which in turn has a positive effect on the local economy. The new calculation will also replace the additional income tax exemption for pensions and the current reimbursement of low-wage earners, as they will remain within the tax-free threshold in the future. Pensioners who are entitled to the benefit of a living pensioner can be calm because no one is going to take away the EUR 115 promised from them, which has been guaranteed by the state. Death grants will be restored, which have been abolished during the 2009 budget cuts. A total funeral allowance of EUR 280 will be paid regardless of someone's income, for which the state will allocate money specifically to local governments. A number of changes await parents. In order to have more children in Estonia, the support of a family rich in children is increased from 100 euros to 300 euros per month, from 200 euros to 300 euros from the third child and from 370 to 400 children from the seventh. The student loan for the parents of a child with severe disability shall be cancelled. The good news is that it is still possible to make joint tax returns. The lack of young teachers is a major problem in the Estonian education system. University graduates have no interest in going to work at school because the teacher's salary is not attractive. Coalition parties have taken the utmost care to raise teachers' salaries to 120% of the national average wage, such as those suitable for graduate workers. The necessary amendments to this end shall be made gradually. The current draft budget, or rather the amendments, already allocates additional resources to the increase in teachers' salaries, while also supporting the increase in the pay of kindergarten teachers. The abolition of the 0.5% reduction in the social tax rate does not disturb businesses, as the revenue from it would be marginal, but the addition of EUR 41 million to the sickness fund is much more gratifying, because it affects our people most directly. The exclusion of special preferential corporate tax on the health promotion costs offered to their employees makes it possible to offer these opportunities for EUR 400 per employee per year, which is EUR 100 per quarter. There has been a significant change in the attitude towards agriculture and paying the support needed to survive the farmers' crisis. The plan is to restore the transitional support, which will be paid EUR 19.9 million next year. From next year, crisis aid funds will also be increased by EUR 0.5 million, allowing support for both breeding and pig farming. For tourism companies, the positive news is that the planned increase in VAT for accommodation establishments will be avoided in the coming year. Tourism companies have made numerous appeals so that tax increases do not occur. It can now finally be said that this will not happen, and the tourism sector will not have to fear that their position will be undermined by the state in already harsh conditions of competition. Finally, the investments included in the amendments proposed by all the political groups will support regional projects amounting to EUR 4.1 million. There are other changes that contribute to prosperity, but even the above should be sufficient justification for supporting the Bill 302 at today's reading. Thank you.

ParlaMint-EE_2016-12-14_U576	Ladies and gentlemen, I would first of all like to thank those members of the Riigikogu who were able to remain balanced in their speeches today, arguing and thinking with two hemispheres during their speech. This means that if we lower taxes, raise certain subsidies, increase benefits, then we also need to find a source of revenue somewhere. This balanced analysis, weighing both sides, has occurred here. These were credible, meaningful speeches. I agree with almost everything that Mr Herkel, for example, and several other colleagues, said. However, unfortunately, I have to say that I cannot agree with the speeches made by almost any previous coalition partner in the Reform Party, where there was no comprehensive analysis. I came here completely unprepared, driven by previous speeches, and quickly sought out some materials to clarify the facts for the verbatim report. First of all, I'll give you my opinion. In my opinion, the re-independence has never existed in Estonia and is not currently subject to proportionate income tax. There has always been progressive income tax. This artificial theme, with which two major parties, the Reform Party and the Centre Party have been opposed over 25 years and fooled by voters, does not correspond to reality. After the restoration of independence, there was progressive income tax in Estonia: the tax-free rate was 40 kroons, initially 400 roubles per month. The steps were divided as follows: up to 80 kroons per month, 16%, 80.150 kroons per month, 24%, over 150 kroons per month, 33%. From 1 August 1992, the tax was 50% for the part of income exceeding 200 kroons per month. Further, the major changes entered into force on 1 January 1994. The rate of income tax was 26%, the rate of tax-free income was initially 300 kroons per month. Later, the kroons have been replaced by euros and the income tax rate has been reduced from 26% to 20 percent and the income tax-free minimum has been raised. At the same time, I would like to emphasise that, at the request of the Reform Party, complex and bureaucratic income tax refunds for low-paid people have been applied instead of the much simpler low-paid income tax exemptions recommended by the Fatherland and Res Publica Union. I would stress that the income tax exemption was replaced by a tax refund, which was in fact much more bureaucratic and complicated. So I would like to say that there is no proportional income tax in Estonia, so it is not possible to lose it or to switch to the progressive income tax that we have been in place all along. That is the first clarification that I wanted to say to my colleagues. Such a linguistic dispute, I think, could remain in the past and we could focus more on the content of which deciles, which groups, social groups win or lose with certain tax changes and which do not, which motivates more to earn, how to grab more hands on the labour market, etc. Let's put an end to these linguistic disputes! Second clarification. The young gentleman behind me, who is running our meeting right now, said that juices are also taxed on sweets. First of all, we don't have a bill for that yet. Secondly, European Union law, however we wish, does not allow it. Even if the Estonian state wanted to do so, it would not receive such a distinction that the juices could be taxed with sweet tax, because the European Union has regulated, which are juices, which are nectars and which are juice drinks. Juices are those that can only contain natural sugar, i.e. no water should be added. Juice is a completely natural beverage sold in a shop, it is not possible to add sugar, even less can be taxed without adding sugar. So to frighten the Estonian people with such examples or ignorance coming from this remote control should not be. I wanted to clarify that. Ladies and gentlemen, Finally. There has been talk of coalition governments, previous and present, and the change of government very passionately. Coalition governments are like living together that ends when jealousy exceeds love. That's what happened to the last coalition. Today, when I listened to these passionate speeches from this speaker, I realized: I also love a loser. Let this be a consolation to the Reform Party. Thank you.
